# OpenL Tablets Security Findings

## 1. Summary

### Audit scope

| Item | Value |
| --- | --- |
| Commit SHA and commit date | `7015c25d68fc8344c53ef846db1e9e0f60e1ec5c`; 2026-09-28T17:30:08+02:00; OpenL Tablets 6.5.0-SNAPSHOT at `pom.xml:7` |
| Audit date | 2026-10-06T01:40:23Z |
| Method | Passive, read-only analysis of HEAD of `main` at the audited base on the assigned branch, with no service started or vulnerability reproduced and the network guard in force (not degraded), an inherited seccomp user-notification guard with private namespaces that ran offline commands in a private network namespace and pinned each download-mode step to its allowed hosts in a private mount namespace; permitted hosts were the Maven repositories `repo.maven.apache.org`, `build.shibboleth.net` and `repository.jboss.org`, the npm registry `registry.npmjs.org` and the scanner data sources `mirror.gcr.io`, `nvd.nist.gov`, `www.cisa.gov`, `raw.githubusercontent.com` and `dependency-check.github.io`, plus the toolchain hosts `github.com`, `release-assets.githubusercontent.com`, `api.adoptium.net`, `archive.apache.org` and `nodejs.org` only for a pinned download of a tool absent from the host, and no toolchain host was contacted because no `tool-*` download step ran (the JDK, Maven, Trivy, Node.js and npm all came from local archives); this run's guard records show that the only outbound connections were the D2 Dependency-Check data-feed update to `nvd.nist.gov` and `dependency-check.github.io` and npm audit to `registry.npmjs.org`, all on TCP 443 to pinned addresses, while the D1 Trivy database download, the dependency tree and Dependency-Check aggregate made no outbound connection and no listener was opened; on case-insensitive file systems `docs/security/` resolves into `Docs/security/`. |
| Audit artifacts | `AUDIT_WORK = /tmp/blitzy/scratch/eeba26fa-effd-4f48-a608-0796922ed137/dest/aw`, outside the working tree: `commands.log`, the per-command guard records and pinned hosts files under `net/`, the guard self-test results, tool versions and archive digests, `deptree/tree.txt`, the Dependency-Check database, its read-only copy and reports, the Trivy cache, report and SBOM probes, the npm audit report, the JGit disassembly, H2 export and fork metadata under `evidence/`, and the merge and check scripts with their outputs; the records of the earlier executions (audit dates 2026-10-05T17:51:40Z and 2026-10-05T20:28:13Z) were not preserved, so every run-derived value, count, timestamp, contact statement and `$AUDIT_WORK` citation in this document comes from the re-execution of the same commands that this directory records, and nothing here relies on the earlier records |
| Tools run: JDK | Eclipse Temurin 27+35, local archive, SHA-256 1cf69a4848ffb728b3b260dfd45206a51566ab571a02a30092271d4c580bccbc; Maven/H2/javap only |
| Tools run: Maven | Apache Maven 3.9.12, local archive, publisher SHA-512 matched and SHA-256 fa2c9948729296c23afd18fd01a90f62cdda09a46191b54a8bc3764c2eee812e; direct goals only |
| Tools run: dependency tree | maven-dependency-plugin 3.11.0, installed in local Maven repository; 86 modules, compile/runtime/provided/test scopes retained |
| Tools run: Dependency-Check | 13.0.0, installed in local Maven repository; NVD API/Cache Last Checked 2026-10-06T01:40:28Z, NVD API/Cache Last Modified 2026-10-05T20:00:04-04; CISA/RetireJS/hosted-suppression timestamps not exposed in `scanInfo` |
| Tools run: Trivy | 0.74.0, local archive, SHA-256 2ae6fe3ee734b7fdf11335663e18c75ea12dccc76062f09f164a3b0f8be4371a; DB v2 `UpdatedAt` 2026-10-05T13:07:51.292695513Z, source `mirror.gcr.io/aquasec/trivy-db:2`; Java index DB not used |
| Tools run: Node.js | 24.21.0, local archive, SHA-256 6e1db87ef58b8819e5d5402eff1536491b18edd8eb7bee5ef7897876e88dc5ff; used only for npm audit |
| Tools run: npm | 12.0.2, local Maven-plugin archive; `auditReportVersion` 2, live registry advisory lookup at 2026-10-06T01:41:19Z; exit 1 means advisories reported, not scan failure |
| Tools run: H2 | 2.4.240, installed Dependency-Check dependency; read-only queries of a private copy of the successfully updated database |
| Tools run: Python | 3.13.7, installed host interpreter run as `python3 -I -S` (isolated mode, standard library only, no virtual environment); merge, recount, guard, checksum and check scripts |
| Tools run: Git | 2.51.0, installed host tool; HEAD-only history queries, commit metadata and boundary evidence |
| Tools run: unshare | util-linux 2.41, installed host tool; private network namespace (`-rn`) for every offline command and private mount namespace (`-rm`) pinning each download-mode step to its allowed hosts |
| Tools run: cURL | 8.14.1, installed host tool; not used: no JDK, Maven, Trivy or Node.js was absent from the host, so no toolchain download step ran |
| Tools run: Linux kernel | 6.12.85+, host kernel; seccomp user notification with the continue flag and `pidfd_getfd`, which the network guard requires |
| Classification | CVE statuses are per resolved occurrence; Open means version-applicable without a verified exclusion, not a reproduced exploit; weakness CVSS v3.1 reflects shipped/default exposure with stated configuration preconditions |
| Database limitations | NVD CPE matches and shaded dependencies were reviewed; fork database absence is not assurance, unknown fixed metadata earns no exemption, and no upstream Git history was fetched |
| Disclosure policy | `SECURITY.md:13-56`, unchanged and cited rather than restated; the passive, read-only method above governs what this register contains |
| Change boundary | two files added |

## 4. Finding Details

### DEP-01

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Micrometer 1.17.1 resolves outside CVE-2026-40984 and CVE-2026-40983; the companion affects probe versions 1.15.11/1.16.5, not parent 1.14.14. |
| Where (file:line) | `pom.xml:93-103`, `pom.xml:451-460`, `/root/.m2/repository/org/springframework/ldap/spring-ldap-core/3.3.8/spring-ldap-core-3.3.8.pom:70-72` |
| Why it matters | Removing the pin restores parent 1.14.14 and CVE-2026-40984; attributing companion 40983 mitigation to that pin would contradict the observed parent range. |
| Preconditions | Compile-scope dependency through Spring Security LDAP and Spring LDAP, even when instrumentation is not selected. |
| Fix direction | Retain the pin until the parent manages a fixed version; Spring LDAP 4.x requires Spring 7, so that parent transition needs platform compatibility work. |
| Default vs production | 40984 is Mitigated-by-pin; 40983 is Not-applicable under the two-fact pin rule, and no Open remediation is assigned. |

### DEP-02

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | The at.yawk.lz4 fork resolves 1.11.3 instead of Kafka’s affected 1.10.2, mitigating CVE-2026-59949. |
| Where (file:line) | `pom.xml:119-121`, `pom.xml:871-875`, `/root/.m2/repository/org/apache/kafka/kafka-clients/4.3.1/kafka-clients-4.3.1.pom:24-26` |
| Why it matters | Deleting the override restores a vulnerable compression dependency. |
| Preconditions | Kafka client compressed data processing is used. |
| Fix direction | Retain the managed override until Kafka declares a fixed release; do not substitute unrelated org.lz4 advisory coordinates. |
| Default vs production | The runtime dependency is fixed in the shipped reactor; application authentication does not replace a dependency pin. |

### DEP-03

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | JGit core/LFS fork 7.8.0.202609011348-openl2 diverges from seed 7.3.0; CVE-2014-9390, CVE-2023-4759 and CVE-2025-4949 are version-inapplicable. |
| Where (file:line) | `pom.xml:153`, `pom.xml:774-783`, `$AUDIT_WORK/evidence/fork-metadata.txt`, `$AUDIT_WORK/evidence/jgit-bytecode/DirCacheCheckout.javap.txt:3683-3737`, `$AUDIT_WORK/evidence/jgit-bytecode/ManifestParser.javap.txt:163-206` |
| Why it matters | Upstream identity alone does not establish which fixes a private fork retains; SHA-256 is 2aceeb26dab22a581589932e464db0a77a43c6bb1a1cff4932e01b003963ddc1. |
| Preconditions | Fork SCM points at upstream JGit, japicmp baseline is 7.7.0.202606012155-r and manifest `Bundle-Version` is 7.8.1.202609231022. |
| Fix direction | Retain verified 2014 path checks and 2025 parser protections; 2023 patch presence remains unverified, and no claim that all upstream fixes are present is made. |
| Default vs production | These three advisories are outside the declared upstream base’s affected ranges; a scanner’s fork-coordinate blind spot still limits assurance. |

### DEP-04

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Frozen org.openl:flyway-core 4.2.0.3 derives from org.flywaydb:flyway-parent 4.2.0; no advisory was returned by either database. |
| Where (file:line) | `pom.xml:801-807`, `/root/.m2/repository/org/openl/flyway-core/4.2.0.3/flyway-core-4.2.0.3.pom:38-51` |
| Why it matters | Frozen upstream maintenance and absent change records limit assurance; database absence is not proof that no CVE exists. |
| Preconditions | The fork manages standalone security database migrations. |
| Fix direction | Review maintenance and licensing before replacing or rebasing the fork; no CVE-driven upgrade is verified by this snapshot. |
| Default vs production | Both environments inherit the fork; this coverage-only seed is Not-applicable and No advisory, not an assurance of safety. |

### DEP-05

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Transitive com.google.code.findbugs:jsr305 3.0.2 supplies `ParametersAreNonnullByDefault` in ten OpenL Studio classes; no advisory was returned by either database. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/rest/acl/resolver/AclRepositoryIdConverter.java:3`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/rest/acl/resolver/AlcSidValueArgumentResolver.java:3`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/converter/AProjectConverter.java:3`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/converter/ProjectIdentityConverter.java:5`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/converter/ProjectStatusConverter.java:3`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/messaging/ProjectSocketNotificationService.java:9`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/service/AbstractProjectService.java:18`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/service/WorkspaceProjectService.java:25`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/validator/NewBranchValidator.java:6`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/validator/NewBranchValidatorFactory.java:4` |
| Why it matters | Legacy annotation ownership and transitive availability need maintenance tracking; absence of an advisory is not proof that none exists. |
| Preconditions | OpenSAML API through Spring Security SAML provides the compile dependency. |
| Fix direction | Keep the transitive annotation contract explicit and assess migration to JSpecify independently of a CVE; no security upgrade is verified here. |
| Default vs production | The resolved annotation dependency is the same in both; this coverage-only seed has No advisory and no Open remediation. |

### DEP-06

### SEC-01

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | JWT authorization exempts every admin path, including deployment. |
| Where (file:line) | `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:83-85`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/RulesDeployerRestController.java:33-123`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:24`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-ruleservice-ws-beans.xml:42`, `compose.yaml:55` |
| Why it matters | Unauthenticated deployment changes executable business rules and production data. |
| Preconditions | The deployer is enabled in the reference stack; the exemption persists when JWT authentication is enabled. |
| Fix direction | Require authentication for deployment, download and delete while keeping the readiness endpoint public. |
| Default vs production | The packaged deployer is off (`ruleservice.deployer.enabled=false`), so its endpoint is not bound; the reference stack enables it, and JWT alone does not protect it. |

### SEC-02

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | OpenL Studio defaults to single mode with anonymous ADMIN, and OpenL Rule Services authentication defaults off. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SingleSecurityConfig.java:27-31`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:23-31`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:59` |
| Why it matters | Remote users inherit administrative access without presenting credentials. |
| Preconditions | Application ports are reachable; no production authentication override is configured. |
| Fix direction | Add independent remote-access switches that preserve current defaults, warn on each insecure condition and refuse non-loopback anonymous requests at the secure values. |
| Default vs production | Zero configuration is unauthenticated; authenticated modes, JWT and private network boundaries reduce production exposure. |

### SEC-03

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Rule expressions resolve unrestricted classpath types through core registrations, imports, caches and dynamic loading. |
| Where (file:line) | `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/conf/TypeResolver.java:177-194`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/conf/TypeResolver.java:346-425`, `DEV/AGENTS.md:111` |
| Why it matters | Untrusted rule authors gain process-level capabilities beyond business-rule evaluation. |
| Preconditions | Rules are authored by an untrusted party; single mode grants the editing principal administrative rights. |
| Fix direction | Human decision: add a policy-preserving system-class switch, propagate it before resolver construction, separate policy cache keys and enforce method/field return types. |
| Default vs production | The engine provides no system-class restriction in any deployment; production repository approvals reduce exposure but do not sandbox compilation. |

### SEC-04

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Browser chains omit CSRF protection and the session cookie has no application-configured SameSite attribute. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:28-47`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:55-134`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:54-117`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/webapp/WEB-INF/web.xml:7-13` |
| Why it matters | Ambient credentials authorize unwanted writes and logout. |
| Preconditions | A user visits untrusted content while a session or cached Basic credential is active. |
| Fix direction | Add switched CSRF/cookie policy with eager cookie-token issuance, explicit chain placement, UI token transmission and POST-only local logout; exempt only authenticated PAT/bearer requests and validated SAML protocol messages. |
| Default vs production | Current writes and GET logout remain accepted; secure SAML requires SameSite None with Secure rather than Lax. |

### SEC-05

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | SSO and static chains omit header writers, and no application CSP was found. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SecurityConfig.java:30-58`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:53-134`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:52-117` |
| Why it matters | Framing and unrestricted content sources weaken browser isolation. |
| Preconditions | The browser can reach OpenL Studio without compensating proxy headers. |
| Fix direction | Add independent all-chain-header and CSP switches, startup warnings and per-chain response tests without breaking current embedding by default. |
| Default vs production | Form-based chains inherit Spring headers; SSO/static chains need application or proxy coverage. |

### SEC-06

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | SAML and OIDC use only session registration after authentication, not session-ID rotation. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:65-68`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:224`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:188` |
| Why it matters | A previously known session identifier retains its relationship to an authenticated session. |
| Preconditions | SSO is selected and an existing session is established before login. |
| Fix direction | Compose session-ID rotation with registration and retain saved SSO state; test identifier change and session registry continuity. |
| Default vs production | Single mode has no SSO login, and form-based authentication has its own Spring session handling. |

### SEC-07

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | ENC uses AES-128-CBC, zero IV and unsalted SHA-1 key derivation without an integrity tag; only password-suffixed properties are encrypted, only when `secret.key` and `secret.cipher` are both nonblank, and decode failure returns an empty string. |
| Where (file:line) | `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PassCoder.java:23-24`, `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/PassCoder.java:71-76`, `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:138-220` (seed: lines 139–220), `DEV/org.openl.spring/src/org/openl/spring/env/DynamicPropertySource.java:190-197`, `DEV/org.openl.spring/resources/openl-default.properties:12`, `DEV/org.openl.spring/resources/openl-default.properties:35`, `DEV/org.openl.spring/resources/openl-default.properties:38` |
| Why it matters | Settings disclosure or undetected modification exposes secrets and silently changes configuration. |
| Preconditions | Settings can be read or altered; effective encryption depends on a nonblank key and cipher. |
| Fix direction | Standalone fix: add ENC2 authenticated encryption with random IV and salted PBKDF2-HMAC-SHA256 derivation at 600000 iterations, explicit errors naming the property key on both the `DynamicPropertySource` and `ApplicationPropertySource` decode paths, and offline re-encryption tooling that refuses linked or concurrently changed files while preserving ENC decoding and warning on its failures. ENC2 requires a non-blank `secret.key`, so the environment load at format ENC2, saving, ENC2 decoding and conversion fail explicitly without one, and a format other than exactly ENC or ENC2 is refused. |
| Default vs production | With zero configuration `secret.key` resolves to the empty `repository.encode.decode.key`, so `save` encrypts no saved value and passwords stay in plain text; automatic password encryption needs a nonblank `secret.key` with `secret.cipher`, and once a key is set, production secret management and the proposed ENC2 format reduce settings-file exposure. |

### SEC-08

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Internal passwords have no configured minimum and a 25-character maximum; multi mode still rejects blank passwords. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/rest/validation/InternalPasswordConstraintValidator.java:25-36` |
| Why it matters | Short passwords remain valid while longer passphrases are rejected. |
| Preconditions | Internal-user creation is enabled in multi mode. |
| Fix direction | Introduce minimum/maximum switches preserving current validation, warn below secure lengths and test blank handling and boundary values. |
| Default vs production | Single mode has no password login; production IdP policy can replace local password management. |

### SEC-09

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | PAT creation accepts null expiry and has no maximum future lifetime. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/service/PatGeneratorServiceImpl.java:61-86` |
| Why it matters | A disclosed token remains reusable until manually revoked. |
| Preconditions | A PAT has been issued and its secret is disclosed. |
| Fix direction | Add a maximum-lifetime switch preserving optional expiry and return a stable 400 response for secure-policy violations. |
| Default vs production | The shipped single-mode default issues and accepts no PATs, because PAT support loads only when `user.mode` is not `single`; every hardened authenticated mode accepts PATs with no maximum lifetime, and no property caps it today. |

| Field | Content |

| What | External authority names are expanded through matching OpenL groups, including ADMIN-bearing groups. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/GetUserPrivileges.java:49-56`, `STUDIO/org.openl.security/src/org/openl/rules/security/Privileges.java:11-18`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/AdminPrivilege.java:32` |
| Why it matters | An IdP group whose name matches an ADMIN-bearing OpenL group gives its members OpenL Studio administration, so IdP group naming and membership, not OpenL user administration, decide who administers OpenL Studio. |
| Preconditions | AD, SAML or OAuth2 is enabled and the IdP supplies a matching privileged group. |
| Fix direction | At the secure switch value, refuse ADMIN from both expanded external groups and raw external authorities while retaining explicit local grants and nonadministrative mappings. |
| Default vs production | Single mode already grants ADMIN; production must also constrain IdP group issuance. |

### SEC-11

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Public endpoints return system/build/configuration information; the HTTP information response includes the caller’s cookies and headers. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/common/SysInfoController.java:26-100`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SecurityConfig.java:43`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/AdminRestController.java:39-140`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:204-209` |
| Why it matters | Anonymous users learn deployment internals, and sensitive request values are reflected into diagnostic responses. |
| Preconditions | Information endpoints are reachable, even when other endpoints require authentication. |
| Fix direction | Add separate OpenL Studio and OpenL Rule Services public-info switches that preserve health checks and existing endpoint names. At the secure value the OpenL Studio static-chain matcher `/rest/public/**` stops covering `sys.json` and `http.json`. OpenL Rule Services then keeps only `/admin/healthcheck/` public under `/admin/` in both independent gates, `RuleServicesFilter.skipAuthorization` and the `/admin/` exemption in `JWTValidator.authorize`, so every other `AdminRestController` information, service-list, error, manifest and configuration path requires a token. |
| Default vs production | Public information is shipped; proxy restriction reduces access without removing the application exemption. |

### SEC-12

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Enabled DB store-log records complete request/response bodies without built-in redaction or retention. |
| Where (file:line) | `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/storelogdata/db/DefaultEntity.java:42-52`, `WSFrontend/AGENTS.md:40` (seed: line 39), `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws.storelogdata.db/resources/openl-default.properties:1` |
| Why it matters | Business and personal data remain available to storage readers longer than required. |
| Preconditions | DB store-log is enabled and requests contain sensitive data. |
| Fix direction | Add independent payload-storage and retention switches, warn for either insecure condition, refuse a negative or malformed retention and test suppressed payloads and aged-record purge. With payloads off the mapper skips the `@Request` and `@Response` elements of the default and every custom entity while collecting them, for REST and Kafka records alike, leaving every other mapped and custom field and the logged data unchanged. A positive retention makes `DBStoreLogDataService` schedule one single-threaded fixed-delay purge, first after a minute and then hourly, that deletes `DefaultEntity` and registered custom-entity records older than the cutoff by their `@IncomingTime` value in bounded batches, refuses to store an entity without a usable timestamp and skips such a registered entity with a WARN, never overlaps runs, logs a failed run and retries it at the next run, and stops at shutdown. |
| Default vs production | Store-log is not enabled by zero configuration; production must govern stored fields and retention explicitly. |

### SEC-13

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | No inbound login throttling or account lockout was found; Git credential back-off protects only outbound Git access. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:24-49`, `compose.yaml:149-199`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.git/resources/openl-default.properties:14-19` |
| Why it matters | Repeated credential guesses are not limited by the application. |
| Preconditions | Multi or AD login is enabled; no proxy or IdP throttling is supplied. |
| Fix direction | Add switched failed-attempt counting and timed lockout, independently of the password-length fix, at the one provider bean both form login and HTTP Basic reach, keyed by the account each mode resolves a name to, in bounded, expiring state that never evicts an active lock, with refusals identical to a wrong password; retain complementary proxy API limits. |
| Default vs production | Single mode has no credential check; the reference proxy has no throttling directives. |

### SEC-14

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Dependency-Check retains its default nonfailing threshold of 11 and the image workflow has no vulnerability exit-code gate. |
| Where (file:line) | `pom.xml:1989-2006`, `.github/workflows/trivy.yml:50-59` |
| Why it matters | Builds publish components with known high-severity advisories without a policy failure. |
| Preconditions | A build retains vulnerable packages; this score represents their potential network exposure, not a scanner entry point. |
| Fix direction | Human decision: make the OWASP threshold command-line-overridable at 7, maintain evidenced expiring exemptions and align Trivy’s explicit HIGH/CRITICAL gate; enable only after the final aggregate result is clean. |
| Default vs production | The artifacts behind the shipped default and a hardened production deployment come from the same report-only Dependency-Check profile and Trivy image workflow, so runtime hardening does not change this build-pipeline exposure. |

### SEC-15

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Successful login timestamps exist, but structured failed-login, denial, PAT and ACL-change auditing was not found. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/LastLoginRecorder.java:23-31`, `STUDIO/org.openl.security.acl/src/org/openl/security/acl/config/EnabledAclConfiguration.java:63`, `STUDIO/org.openl.security.standalone/resources/db/flyway/common/V13.4__Merge_privileges_into_roles.sql:7-12` |
| Why it matters | Reviewers lack a reliable security-event record for investigating privilege changes. |
| Preconditions | Authenticated modes and administrative or token operations are used. |
| Fix direction | Write one secret-free JSON event with bounded, control-character-escaped fields through one dedicated logger at every authentication filter's failure path, in one shared access-denial handler and through class-based advice on the token, ACL and membership services, so token creation, single and per-user token revocation, and ordinary project, repository, root and bulk ACL edits are all recorded. Write each mutation outcome after its transaction completes, write each refusal and each nested change once, and leave every authentication, authorization and mutation result unchanged. Test every boundary's events, committed outcomes, hostile values and secret exclusion. |
| Default vs production | Shipped single mode has no login or ACL enforcement, so the missing events add no exposure beyond [SEC-02](#sec-02) there. Hardened authenticated modes keep only the last successful interactive login time, and no application setting enables failed-login, denial, PAT or ACL-change events. |

### SEC-16

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | The in-house x-forwarded-filter evaluates forwarded headers without a trusted-peer boundary. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/webapp/WEB-INF/web.xml:33-44` (seed: lines 34–43), `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/servlet/RuleServicesFilter.java:69-99`, `pom.xml:482-490` |
| Why it matters | Caller-controlled origin/address information affects redirects and proxy-derived security decisions. |
| Preconditions | Application ports are reachable directly, or a proxy forwards untrusted header values. |
| Fix direction | Add trusted-proxy switches preserving wildcard trust by default and use the original peer address before wrapping requests. |
| Default vs production | The reference proxy exists but application ports remain published; production must isolate ports and overwrite incoming headers. |

| Field | Content |

### SEC-18

| Field | Content |

| Preconditions | The reference stack is used without an external TLS ingress. |

### SEC-19

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Kafka invocation has no OpenL-layer authorization check or secure transport/authentication default. |
| Where (file:line) | `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/kafka-deploy-default.yaml:1-19`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:152-170`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:5`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:26-27` |
| Why it matters | A broker-authorized producer can invoke rules without the HTTP JWT policy. |
| Preconditions | Kafka publishing is enabled and broker authentication or topic ACLs are absent. |
| Fix direction | Require broker SASL or mutual TLS and principal-specific topic ACLs. Set `security.protocol` with matching SASL or TLS client settings in each of the `consumer.configs`, `producer.configs` and `dlt.producer.configs` maps of every deployed `kafka-deploy.yaml`, as defined at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/kafka-deploy-default.yaml:1`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/kafka-deploy-default.yaml:8`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/kafka-deploy-default.yaml:14`. Service- and method-level maps extend and override these top-level defaults, as `Docs/user-guides/rule-services/configuration.md:625-629` states, so every override keeps the same protocol and authentication. |
| Default vs production | REST is the only default publisher (`ruleservice.publishers=RESTFUL`, used when a project's `rules-deploy.xml` names no publisher); enabling Kafka requires independent infrastructure controls. |

### SEC-20

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | The reference stack enables wildcard CORS, public JDWP/database ports, a fixed DB password and an admin-login hint. |
| Where (file:line) | `compose.yaml:11-23`, `compose.yaml:39-57`, `compose.yaml:93-101`, `compose.yaml:136`, `compose.yaml:236` |

| Default vs production | These are reference-stack exposures, not unavoidable product defaults; production must supply private ports and generated secrets. |

### SEC-21

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | The deployer and `openl:deploy` write directly to production without OpenL Studio’s branch/ACL workflow or a release approval. |
| Where (file:line) | `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/RulesDeployerRestController.java:33-128`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.deployer/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/deployer/RulesDeployerService.java:123-146`, `Util/openl-maven-plugin/src/org/openl/rules/maven/DeployMojo.java:18-71`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/deployment/service/DeploymentServiceImpl.java:137-180` |
| Why it matters | Repository permissions alone do not enforce approved rule promotion. |
| Preconditions | A caller has deployment or production-repository credentials; the reference stack additionally enables the unauthenticated deployer. |
| Fix direction | Enforce Git approvals and least-privilege CI deployment credentials; disable the deployer on reachable nodes. |
| Default vs production | The shipped default disables the deployer REST API, whose property comment limits it to development use (`WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:22-24`), while `RulesDeployerService` is documented for deploying to a production repository without OpenL Studio (`Docs/user-guides/rule-services/appendices/programmatic-deployment.md:3-5`) and `openl:deploy` calls it directly (`Util/openl-maven-plugin/src/org/openl/rules/maven/DeployMojo.java:68-70`). Neither the deployer nor `openl:deploy` applies OpenL Studio’s main-branch check, deployment ACL permissions or any release approval, so production workflow approvals belong to infrastructure. |

### SEC-22

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | The configured SSE algorithm is object metadata rather than a server-side encryption request on saved files, modification markers and history copies. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/src/org/openl/rules/repository/aws/S3Repository.java:276-287`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/src/org/openl/rules/repository/aws/S3Repository.java:482-490`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/src/org/openl/rules/repository/aws/S3Repository.java:523-527`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.repository.aws/resources/openl-default.properties:16-19` |
| Why it matters | The repository does not enforce the encryption policy that its setting describes. |
| Preconditions | The S3 repository is selected and its SSE setting is relied on; storage access or backend protection is relevant. |
| Fix direction | Switch actual SSE requests on for saved objects, modification markers and the `copyObject` request of history copies; add the missing module test root and mocked put and copy request assertions. `S3RepositoryFactory` reads the `sse-request.enabled` setting explicitly and passes it to the repository before initialization, because the generic setter binding would look for `sse-request-enabled`. |
| Default vs production | No SSE algorithm is selected by default; a bucket-enforced encryption policy compensates independently. |

### SEC-23

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Generated RSA-4096 credentials use one key for signing/decryption and a ten-year certificate. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/Migrator.java:103-114` (seed: lines 103–113), `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/install/KeyPairCertUtils.java:39-52`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:43-58` |
| Why it matters | One key compromise spans both functions and an extended rotation window. |
| Preconditions | SAML is enabled; the key is compromised during its lifetime. |
| Fix direction | Add switched independent encryption credentials and shorter certificate validity, keep complete existing key pairs and published metadata compatibility, and, at the secure setting, refuse incomplete, mismatched or undecodable pairs and a decryption pair that reuses the signing key with errors naming their settings. |
| Default vs production | Single mode generates no SAML key; production rotation and protected key storage reduce the risk. |

### SEC-24

| Where (file:line) | `Dockerfile:181-182` |
| Why it matters | Parsing accepted form content can exhaust process resources. |

### SEC-25

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Permission removal clears the cache before commit but lacks the existing after-completion eviction used on other mutation paths. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/cache2k.xml:17-18`, `STUDIO/org.openl.security.acl/src/org/openl/security/acl/repository/SimpleRepositoryAclServiceImpl.java:244-255`, `STUDIO/org.openl.security.acl/src/org/openl/security/acl/repository/SimpleRepositoryAclServiceImpl.java:277-298`, `STUDIO/org.openl.security.acl/src/org/openl/security/acl/repository/SimpleRepositoryAclServiceImpl.java:307-331`, `STUDIO/org.openl.security.acl/src/org/openl/security/acl/repository/SimpleRepositoryAclServiceImpl.java:79-95` |
| Why it matters | A concurrent reader can retain an old granting ACL until the two-minute expiry. |
| Preconditions | ACL mode is enabled and a read repopulates the cache while the revocation transaction is uncommitted. |
| Fix direction | Reuse post-commit invalidation on every revocation path, including affected descendants, and test transaction timing with controlled cache reads. |
| Default vs production | Single mode bypasses ACL; current local eviction limits exposure but does not close the commit race. |

### SEC-26

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Dispatcher servlet multipart registration sets both file and request size to unlimited. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/servlet/SpringInitializer.java:153-158` |
| Why it matters | Container parsing occurs before downstream upload validation and can consume unbounded resources. |
| Preconditions | Multipart endpoints are reachable without container/ingress limits. |

### SEC-27

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | The run-result XLSX export has no row cap, because its `Integer.MAX_VALUE` argument only sets how many tests share a sheet, and it materializes the whole workbook in a byte array. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/projects/rest/controller/ProjectsRunController.java:183-188`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ResultExport.java:39-64`, `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/testmethod/export/ParameterExport.java:254-289` |
| Why it matters | Large result exports can exhaust the OpenL Studio heap despite bounded upload intake. |
| Preconditions | A run has produced enough rows and XLSX output is requested; single mode imposes no login requirement. |
| Fix direction | Add the row-cap switch `test.run.export.max-rows` (default `-1`, unlimited as today; secure `100000`), whose cap counts every row the export emits across its result and parameter sheets, header rows and expanded nested values included. A bounded preflight that shares the exporter's own row layout counts those rows before the workbook is built, refuses an export above the cap with an explicit error naming the property and lets one at the cap export unchanged. A blank, non-integer or out-of-`int`-range value is refused when the controller is constructed, before any export. |
| Default vs production | Upload caps do not bound output; production limits and authenticated access reduce the residual #952 exposure. |

### SEC-28

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | `oauth2` login keeps the access and refresh tokens in an `InMemoryOAuth2AuthorizedClientService` store that no OpenL code reads or clears. The common `ProviderManager` disables credential erasure only for the SAML, OAuth2 login and bearer filters, and erasure clears neither these tokens nor the SAML response. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:45-47`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:181-187`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OpenLOAuth2UserService.java:33-35`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:217-222`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2AccessTokenConfiguration.java:40-43`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/OpenLResponseAuthenticationConverter.java:69-71`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:24-49` |
| Why it matters | Exposed application memory yields reusable OAuth2 access and refresh tokens, not identity only, which outlive logout and session expiry. |
| Preconditions | `oauth2` mode and access to application memory are required. Form login and HTTP Basic in `multi` and `ad` modes keep no password: their chains authenticate through managers that erase it, never through the common manager whose erasure the SAML global-logout reason at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:46` turns off. |
| Fix direction | Stop retaining the OAuth2 access and refresh tokens no OpenL code reads, keeping the ID token OIDC logout uses and the state SAML global logout needs. Keep the password erasure of the form login and HTTP Basic chains and test the OAuth2 login filter's token handling. |
| Default vs production | The shipped `single` mode holds no credential, and `saml` keeps the signed IdP response in `Saml2Authentication`, whose reuse past the provider's validity checks was not established. Production limits memory access and token lifetimes, but no control in the tree removes the OAuth2 tokens; the ID token kept for OIDC logout is a functional need, not a control. |

### SEC-29

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | In `saml` and `oauth2` modes the REST chains start with the shared `SecurityContextPersistenceFilter`, whose session repository saves the non-transient PAT authentication `PatAuthenticationFilter` sets, creating a session when none exists. A later request with only that cookie is authenticated from the stored context without PAT revocation, expiry or account checks. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:32-35`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:113-117`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:97-102`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/filter/PatAuthenticationFilter.java:68-72`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/filter/PatAuthenticationFilter.java:93-97`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/model/PatAuthenticationToken.java:15-19`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/service/PatValidationServiceImpl.java:88-105`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/service/PatAuthServiceImpl.java:63-70` |
| Why it matters | Revoking or expiring a PAT, or disabling or locking its account, leaves the derived session's read and write access in place, with the privileges captured at the PAT request. |
| Preconditions | `saml` or `oauth2` mode, one valid PAT request, and reuse of its session cookie before session expiry. The `multi` and `ad` REST chain (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:24-34`) saves a context only explicitly, and the stored token keeps its PAT administration denial. |
| Fix direction | Keep PAT authentication request-scoped, so no PAT request creates or writes a session context, and refuse PAT contexts already stored in a session. Genuine SSO sessions keep working, and the PAT denials (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/AdminPrivilege.java:31-34`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/NotPatAuth.java:31`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/AuthorizationExpressions.java:24-26`) keep applying per PAT request. |
| Default vs production | The shipped `single` mode (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:31`) has no PAT path, so exposure starts when `saml` or `oauth2` is selected. Short session and PAT lifetimes narrow the window, but no control in the tree revalidates a derived session. |

### SEC-30

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | The login success handler shared by `saml` and `oauth2` login follows the `from` parameter without an application same-origin check. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/CommonAuthenticationConfig.java:71-75`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/SamlSecurityConfig.java:218-223`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:183-189` |
| Why it matters | Users are redirected away from the trusted login origin. |
| Preconditions | A `saml` or `oauth2` mode login receives an off-site target. |
| Fix direction | Accept only normalized same-origin relative redirects, retain saved-request fallback and test rejected off-site targets. |
| Default vs production | The shipped `single` mode has no login redirect, and `multi` and `ad` form login use Spring's default success handler, which ignores `from`. Only `saml` and `oauth2` logins use the common handler, so production deployments in those modes stay exposed until the code fix ships. |

### SEC-31

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Form-based session management explicitly configures an unlimited session count. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/FormBasedAuthenticationConfig.java:45-47` |
| Why it matters | Authenticated session accumulation increases memory and prolongs old-session exposure. |
| Preconditions | Multi or AD login is enabled and a valid user creates concurrent sessions. |

| Default vs production | Single mode has no login; production caps and proxy limits reduce authenticated resource consumption. |

### SEC-32

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | When selected, the supported `noop` value of `security.password.encoder` stores internal-user passwords and personal access token secrets in plain text in the database, because one encoder bean hashes both. This path is distinct from `ENC` settings encryption ([SEC-07](#sec-07)) and in-memory credential retention ([SEC-28](#sec-28)). |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/PasswordEncoderConfig.java:38-42`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/PasswordEncoderConfig.java:56-59`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:5`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/service/PatGeneratorServiceImpl.java:76-82`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/service/UserManagementService.java:99`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/service/UserManagementService.java:186-188` |
| Why it matters | A reader of the user and token tables or their backups would obtain reusable passwords and tokens rather than hashes, and with them those users' OpenL Studio access. |
| Preconditions | An operator explicitly sets `security.password.encoder` to `noop`, and an unauthorized party can read the database or backups. Internal-user passwords exist only in `multi` mode (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/MultiSecurityConfig.java:20-26`), and personal access tokens in every mode except `single` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/pat/config/PatSecurityConfiguration.java:48`). |
| Fix direction | Keep bcrypt as the shipped default with the existing startup warning, and reserve `noop` for tests, as `ITEST/pom.xml:118-120` does, never removing that mode. A deployment that selected `noop` returns to bcrypt by resetting passwords and reissuing tokens, because each algorithm verifies only its own values (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:4`). |
| Default vs production | The shipped default `bcrypt` hashes both credential types, so no plaintext credential reaches the database. A hardened production deployment keeps bcrypt, the control that neutralizes this path; selecting `noop` removes it, with a startup warning that the mode is for tests only. |

### SEC-33

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | OIDC discovery fetches issuer metadata from `security.oauth2.issuer-uri` whatever its scheme, and the advertised authorization, token, JWK Set, userinfo, end-session and introspection endpoints are used as given. No HTTPS policy applies to the issuer or any discovered endpoint. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2Configuration.java:31-35`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2Configuration.java:48-54`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2ImportSelector.java:20-25`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/LazyClientRegistrationRepository.java:32-40`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2JwtAccessTokenConfiguration.java:38-40`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/UserInfoOpaqueTokenIntrospector.java:52-55`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/OAuth2SecurityConfig.java:163-171`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/Oauth2LogoutSuccessHandler.java:26-29`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:246` |
| Why it matters | A plain-HTTP issuer or endpoint exposes the client secret, authorization codes and tokens in transit. Unprotected metadata also lets substituted signing keys and endpoints be trusted, undermining authentication integrity and availability. |
| Preconditions | `oauth2` mode with an `http:` issuer or an identity provider advertising an `http:` endpoint, and a party on the network path to the identity provider. |
| Fix direction | Add the switch `security.oauth2.allow-insecure-endpoints` (default `true`, secure `false`); the secure value requires an absolute `https:` issuer before discovery and absolute `https:` endpoints before any request carrying the client secret, a code or a token. A refusal fails registration with an explicit error naming the property and metadata field, never the secret, and each insecure condition logs a startup WARN. Mocked-fetcher tests cover both states and malformed and refused values. |
| Default vs production | The shipped `single` mode with an empty issuer never contacts an identity provider. Production `oauth2` deployments whose issuer and advertised endpoints all use HTTPS avoid the exposure, but OpenL Studio does not enforce it. |

### SEC-34

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Opaque-token introspection checks token activity on every request but takes the mapped OpenL privileges from a `userInfoOAuth2Cache` entry that keeps, for 30 minutes per token hash, the `privilegeMapper` result built from copies of the local grants. No user, group or privilege change evicts that cache, and only a context refresh clears it. SEC-25 is the separate ACL-cache race. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/UserInfoOpaqueTokenIntrospector.java:63-82`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/UserInfoClaimsConverter.java:55-58`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/cache2k.xml:28-30`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2OpaqueAccessTokenConfiguration.java:22-36`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/GetUserPrivileges.java:27-56`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/service/PrivilegesEvaluator.java:30-45`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/service/GroupManagementService.java:41-47`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/service/GroupManagementService.java:65-69`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/service/UserManagementService.java:192-219`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/config/SpringCachingConfig.java:21-30` |
| Why it matters | A user whose ADMIN privilege or group membership was removed can still read and change what it protected on REST calls with an already introspected token for up to 30 minutes. |
| Preconditions | `user.mode=oauth2`, an issuer whose metadata publishes an introspection endpoint (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OAuth2ImportSelector.java:24-28`), and REST calls with an opaque bearer token whose user loses a local grant while the token stays active. The window ends earlier when the token expires, because an inactive token fails introspection. |
| Fix direction | Cache only the userinfo claims and external authorities, map the local OpenL privileges on every request as the JWT access-token path does at `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/oauth2/OpenLJwtGrantedAuthoritiesConverter.java:24-27`, and keep per-request introspection. Test a grant removal before cache expiry. |
| Default vs production | The shipped `user.mode=single` (`STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:31`) and JWT access tokens do not use this path. Production `oauth2` deployments with opaque tokens stay exposed until the fix ships, and a shorter cache expiry only narrows the window. |

### SEC-35

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | In `saml` mode the IdP metadata location is opened through a generic Spring resource loader with no scheme policy, so plain-HTTP metadata is accepted. Its verification certificates and endpoints are trusted unless `security.saml.server-certificate` pins a certificate; secondary CWE-295. |
| Where (file:line) | `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:52-58`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/studio/security/saml/LazyInMemoryRelyingPartyRegistrationRepository.java:70-83`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/webstudio/web/admin/security/SAMLAuthenticationSettings.java:36-41`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:214`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:228-229` |
| Why it matters | Substituted verification certificates let forged assertions sign in as any OpenL Studio user, administrators included, exposing and altering all projects and settings. With a pin, unauthenticated metadata endpoints still control SSO sign-in and logout. |
| Preconditions | `saml` mode, a plain-HTTP or otherwise unauthenticated metadata location, and a network position between OpenL Studio and the metadata host. Inbound TLS ([SEC-18](#sec-18)) and SP-key reuse ([SEC-23](#sec-23)) do not cover this channel. |
| Fix direction | Add `security.saml.allow-insecure-metadata` (default `true`, today's behavior; secure `false`), under which startup and the SAML settings save refuse, before any fetch, every location other than an absolute `https:` URL or a local `file:` resource, naming both properties. `https:` keeps the JDK trust store, the certificate pin keeps working in both states and tests never open a remote location. |
| Default vs production | The shipped `single` mode with an empty metadata URL and pin leaves the path inactive, while `saml` mode accepts plain-HTTP metadata silently. Hardened deployments use HTTPS or local-file metadata and pin the IdP certificate. |

### SEC-36

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | XPath over a raw `InputSource` reads the uploaded `rules.xml` project name and `rules-deploy.xml` version, with no hardened parser or entity resolver. Under the selected JDK's default configuration (`jdk.xml.dtd.support=allow`, `jdk.xml.jdkcatalog.resolve=continue`, unrestricted external DTD access) that parse loads external DTDs and resolves external entities. Only the ZIP signature is checked first. |
| Where (file:line) | `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.deployer/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/deployer/RulesDeployerService.java:104-109`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.deployer/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/deployer/RulesDeployerService.java:269-273`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.deployer/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/deployer/RulesDeployerService.java:411-414`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.deployer/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/deployer/RulesDeployerService.java:437-448`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/RulesDeployerRestController.java:52-54`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/admin/RulesDeployerRestController.java:69-72`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:24`, `compose.yaml:55` |
| Why it matters | Parsing an uploaded descriptor can disclose local files and internal resources the OpenL Rule Services process reaches; [SEC-01](#sec-01) and [SEC-21](#sec-21) do not cover this XML input. |
| Preconditions | The deployer is enabled and reachable, as in the reference stack. OpenL Rule Services authentication defaults off ([SEC-02](#sec-02)), and the admin-path JWT exemption ([SEC-01](#sec-01)) keeps deployment open with authentication on. |
| Fix direction | Parse each descriptor with a hardened namespace-aware DOM factory (secure processing on; document type declarations, external entities, external DTD loading, XInclude and external schema access off), refuse the deployment through the existing bad-request path when that parse fails, and evaluate XPath on that document. OpenL writes descriptors without a document type declaration and reads them through secure-processing JAXB, which refuses external entities (`DEV/org.openl.rules.project/src/org/openl/rules/project/model/JAXBSerializer.java:22-31`), so no legitimate descriptor changes behavior. |
| Default vs production | The packaged deployer is off (`ruleservice.deployer.enabled=false`); the reference stack enables it. Keeping it off or operator-only narrows who can upload, but nothing in the tree hardens the parse. |

### SEC-37

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | The static Kafka worker executor queues rule work in an unbounded `LinkedBlockingQueue`, but each Kafka service and method configuration runs its own consumer and loop thread, which submits one poll batch, waits until every record of that batch has finished and only then commits and polls again. |
| Where (file:line) | `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:56-61`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:213-225`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:277-278`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/kafka-deploy-default.yaml:2`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaRuleServicePublisher.java:240-304`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:5` |
| Why it matters | Pending work never exceeds the records that the configured consumers' poll batches already hold in memory, so broker traffic cannot grow the queue and no availability impact remains. |
| Preconditions | A larger queue needs operator configuration, more Kafka-published services or method configurations or a larger `max.poll.records`, and message volume alone never enlarges it. |
| Fix direction | No directive is needed: keep the per-batch wait before the next poll, and give the queue an explicit bound in any future change that polls before its batch completes. |
| Default vs production | The shipped default publishes over REST only, and a Kafka deployment inherits a poll cap of 128 records per consumer from the shipped Kafka defaults. A hardened deployment keeps that cap and sizes its number of Kafka-published services to the memory available. |

### SEC-38

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Reply and dead-letter destination topics are accepted from message headers without an allow-list. |
| Where (file:line) | `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:152-170`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:5` |
| Why it matters | Results are routed outside the configured service output topics within the producer’s broker permissions. |
| Preconditions | Kafka is enabled and the OpenL Rule Services principal can publish to the supplied destination. |
| Fix direction | Add the topic allow-list switch `ruleservice.kafka.allowed-reply-topics` (default `*`, secure an explicit list) and route refused headers to configured output/dead-letter topics. An unset key or `*` alone keeps today's routing exactly, a value empty after trimming is the empty list and permits only the configured output and dead-letter topics, and any other value is a comma-separated list of exact topic names, each trimmed, where an empty element, an invalid Kafka topic name or `*` mixed with names fails the deployment of the Kafka-published service with an explicit error naming the property. Outside `*`, a header refused for an empty value, malformed UTF-8 bytes, an invalid topic name or a name outside the allow-set counts as absent, like an absent or null-valued header, in today's order in `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:152-170` (reply header, then output topic; dead-letter header, reply header, then dead-letter topic); an empty configured topic sends nothing, and server-free default and secure tests cover each case. |
| Default vs production | REST defaults (`ruleservice.publishers=RESTFUL`) avoid the path; narrow broker topic ACLs provide a complementary boundary. |

### SEC-39

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | With `log.request-id.header` set, `KafkaService` copies the decoded record header into the MDC `requestId` entry without encoding or a length bound. Plain layouts render that entry through `%X{requestId}` on each log line of the invocation, so CR, LF and other control characters reach the log raw. |
| Where (file:line) | `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:246-248`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:291-300`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/log4j2.properties:10`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/log4j2.properties:18`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:55`, `compose.yaml:58` |
| Why it matters | Any producer of request records controls text on every log line of the invocation, so forged or split entries undermine the integrity of diagnostic and incident logs. [SEC-15](#sec-15) covers OpenL Studio events only and leaves this path unchanged. |
| Preconditions | Kafka publishing is enabled, `log.request-id.header` names a header and a plain layout writes the logs. Any client able to produce to the input topic sets the header ([SEC-19](#sec-19)). |
| Fix direction | Escape control characters and cap the length of the logging representation only, marking a cut value, so a UUID-style ID renders unchanged. Keep the decoded value in reply and dead-letter headers for correlation, and test hostile, overlong, normal and absent headers with mocked Kafka clients. |
| Default vs production | The shipped default leaves `log.request-id.header` empty and publishes over REST only (`ruleservice.publishers=RESTFUL`), and once a header is set the bundled `.war` layouts and the reference stack's `plain` logging render it raw. The image's default ECS JSON format (`Dockerfile:139`) avoids these patterns, and broker topic ACLs narrow who sets the header. |

### SEC-40

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Both dead-letter failure lines of `KafkaService` log the complete request payload that `RequestMessage.asText()` decodes from the record's raw value. Neither line consults the store-log setting, so the [SEC-12](#sec-12) store-log controls cannot prevent this. |
| Where (file:line) | `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:491-496`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaService.java:510-514`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.kafka/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/RequestMessage.java:58-60`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/ser/RequestMessageDeserializer.java:82-87`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/kafka/publish/KafkaRuleServicePublisher.java:298-299`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/log4j2.properties:27-29`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:5` |
| Why it matters | Business and personal request data reach every reader of the application logs and their copies, outside the controls that govern stored payloads. |
| Preconditions | Kafka publishing is enabled, a dead-letter topic is configured or named by a record header, and the dead-letter send fails synchronously or in its callback, for example on a broker, topic or serialization error. Ordinary log access is enough to read the result. |
| Fix direction | Remove the payload from both lines and keep correlation metadata: the dead-letter topic, the record's topic, partition and offset, the request ID through its MDC entry when configured, and the exception. Test synchronous and asynchronous failures with store-log disabled and enabled. |
| Default vs production | REST-only defaults (`ruleservice.publishers=RESTFUL`) do not activate this path. In a Kafka deployment the shipped root logger writes these lines to the console and the rolling file, and a `WARN` or `ERROR` root level or a disabled store-log keeps them. |

### SEC-41

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | Non-HTTPS JWKS locations are read directly, with no remote-scheme policy. |
| Where (file:line) | `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:55-65`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:59`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:66` |
| Why it matters | An unprotected signing-key source undermines token verification trust. |
| Preconditions | JWT is enabled with a remote non-HTTPS JWKS location and an intervening network party exists. |
| Fix direction | Add the scheme-policy switch `ruleservice.authentication.allow-insecure-jwks` (default `true`, secure `false`); secure mode permits HTTPS and local file sources only, tested without opening remote locations. An unset key keeps the shipped `true`, `true` and `false` are read case-insensitively, any other value, blank included, fails construction with an explicit error naming the property, and the refusal of an unset or empty `ruleservice.authentication.jwks` at `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:50-53` stays. At `false`, a location that is not an absolute URI whose scheme, compared case-insensitively, is `https` or `file` fails construction with an explicit error naming both properties before it is opened, `true` keeps today's resolver selection exactly, and the server-free default and secure tests also cover these unset, blank and malformed cases. |
| Default vs production | JWT defaults off (`ruleservice.authentication.enabled=false`) with no JWKS URL (empty `ruleservice.authentication.jwks`); production HTTPS/file configuration avoids transport exposure. |

### SEC-42

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | JWT validation excludes none but accepts every other library-supported algorithm compatible with its keys. |
| Where (file:line) | `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:68-75`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:59` |
| Why it matters | An issuer/key configuration supporting unwanted algorithms has no explicit operator algorithm boundary. |
| Preconditions | JWT is enabled and issuer keys support an algorithm outside the intended policy; signature, issuer and audience checks still apply. |
| Fix direction | Add the algorithm-list switch `ruleservice.authentication.algorithms` (default empty, secure `RS256,RS384,RS512,PS256,PS384,PS512,ES256,ES384,ES512`), where an unset or blank value keeps today's `DISALLOW_NONE` and any other value permits only its trimmed, case-sensitive JWS identifiers from one family, HMAC or asymmetric. An empty element, `none`, an unsupported identifier or a list mixing HMAC with asymmetric names fails construction before the JWKS location is read, and a key set read at construction with no key usable for a listed algorithm also fails construction; each error names the property. Each key verifies only listed algorithms of its own type and curve, a key whose `alg` is outside the list or differs from the token header is never used, and server-free tests cover both states, the refused lists and these mismatches. |
| Default vs production | JWT is off by default (`ruleservice.authentication.enabled=false`); a constrained issuer and asymmetric key configuration reduce exposure without proving an application allow-list. |

### SEC-43

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | A bearer token that fails validation is logged at `WARN` with the whole jose4j exception. jose4j 0.9.7 builds that message from the raw claims JSON on claim failures and from the compact token on parsing, algorithm, key or signature failures, and the console and file layouts write it unredacted. |
| Where (file:line) | `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/src/org/openl/rules/ruleservice/spring/JWTValidator.java:101-107`, `pom.xml:109`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/log4j2.properties:10`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/log4j2.properties:18`, `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice.ws/resources/openl-default.properties:59` |
| Why it matters | Log readers obtain identity claims from tokens refused by a claim check and complete bearer tokens from tokens refused during processing. A valid token refused only because its signing key was unresolvable stays usable until it expires. |
| Preconditions | Authentication is enabled (`ruleservice.authentication.enabled=true`) and a request presents a bearer token that fails validation. Exposure needs read access to the host logs or their collecting platform. |
| Fix direction | Log one bounded, categorized refusal reason derived from jose4j error codes and cause types, never the exception, its message, its cause or the token. Log-capture tests prove each category records no token, claim value or library message text. |
| Default vs production | JWT validation is off by default (`ruleservice.authentication.enabled=false`), so the shipped default logs no token. Deployments that enable it write refused-token content to the ordinary logs, which the store-log controls of [SEC-12](#sec-12) do not cover; restricted log access narrows but never removes that exposure. |

### SEC-44

| Field | Content |
| --- | --- |
| What | The reference proxy mounts the `www_conf` volume as its configuration directory `/etc/nginx`, and its public root aliases that directory. The init service writes the generated nginx configuration beside the landing page there, and the commented TLS lines place the certificate and private key under `/etc/nginx/ssl`. |
| Where (file:line) | `compose.yaml:77-79`, `compose.yaml:193-195`, `compose.yaml:149-199`, `compose.yaml:181-183`, `compose.yaml:202-242`, `compose.yaml:243-246`, `compose.yaml:67-69` |
| Why it matters | Anonymous clients can read the generated configuration, disclosing internal upstream names, ports and routing, and any other readable file there. With the documented TLS lines enabled, the private key sits in the served tree, guarded only by file permissions. |
| Preconditions | The reference compose stack is deployed and its proxy port is reachable; no account is needed. Key exposure also needs the commented TLS lines enabled with the key at the documented path. |
| Fix direction | Serve a dedicated public webroot outside `/etc/nginx`, or none, and keep configuration, certificate and key files out of every served path. The change belongs to the proxy configuration, which the application cannot enforce. |
| Default vs production | Only the reference stack aliases its configuration directory and it ships no private key, so the current exposure is the configuration and any operator-added file. Production proxies outside `compose.yaml` avoid it when their public root excludes those files; [SEC-18](#sec-18) and [SEC-20](#sec-20) track separate exposures. |

## 5. Remediation LOC Estimate

### Sizing basis

| Item | Basis |
| --- | --- |
| Scope | Added plus modified LOC; eleven independent upgrade units and 44 weaknesses; only Confirmed weaknesses and Open dependency groups are estimated |
| Tests | Test LOC is the sum of test methods per state × the assigned reference test's average method length, plus a per-row fixture allowance for scaffold and setup; states are default, secure and partial plus each other case the directive's Tests label requires; averages span the `@Test`, `it(` or `test(` line through the closing brace, string literals ignored, and range 4.71–38.00; rounding rule: the summed method LOC is rounded half-up to an integer before the fixture allowance is added, and a row states any exception; browser (Vitest) and SAML cases are sized as separate case groups; per-finding inputs are in [Test sizing by finding](#test-sizing-by-finding) |
| Shared edits | The shared security test context (`AbstractStudioOpenApiTest` setup extraction, `AbstractStudioMockMvcTest`, `SecurityChainTestConfiguration`) carried by SEC-04, `JWTValidatorTest` JWKS and signing fixture, its log-capture helper and the `JWTValidator` refusal-log catch by SEC-01, Kafka startup fixture and the `KafkaService.createService` `Consumer` and `Producer` test seam by SEC-38, migration header by SEC-02; repeated paths with distinct edits are not duplicate LOC |
| Confidence | Low marks uncertain upgrade/API work; generated lockfile lines are excluded; no bound exceeds four times its low unless confidence is Low |
| Human decision | SEC-03 and SEC-14 remain Open, receive estimates and Project Guide recommendations but no remediation directive; their totals are separate |
| Switch contract | All proposed defaults preserve the audited runtime behavior; each insecure effective condition logs its own startup WARN naming the finding, property and secure value; every switch entry targets 6.6.0 and `docs/security/MIGRATION_NOTES.md` |

### Test sizing by finding

Secure: mocked `putObject` requests for files and modification marker and the mocked history-copy `copyObject` request ask for server-side encryption AES256; the copy request sets no metadata directive in either state, and existing metadata behavior remains documented. |

Secure: two generated keys, `notAfter` 730 days after generation, complete matching pairs retained, incomplete, mismatched or undecodable pairs refused naming their settings, a decryption pair reusing the signing key refused naming its settings. Partial: one property secure, remaining WARN asserted; metadata migration verified. |

Secure: 104857600/209715200. Partial: one negative limit warns. Mock `ServletContext` capture plus advice mapping for container part-limit failure. Secure: results above cap refused with explicit property-naming error; boundary result exports. |

Secure: eleventh login expires oldest session, registry stays consistent. |

Recording loader and local file only; never open a remote URL. |
