Refused: a list mixing HMAC and asymmetric names fails construction before any JWKS location is opened, and a key set read at construction with no key for a listed algorithm fails construction; a token whose header algorithm mismatches the key type, or one only a key with an unlisted or different `alg` verifies, is refused. |

### Human-decision recommendations

| Finding | Recommendation | Verification | Risk and hand-off |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [SEC-03](#sec-03) | Add `rules.block-system-classes=false` to `OpenLSystemProperties` with secure value true, declared in OpenL Studio and OpenL Rule Services core defaults; use the two WARN owners in the switch table. Propagate beside `dispatching.validation` through `WSFrontend/org.openl.rules.ruleservice/resources/openl-ruleservice-core-beans.xml:18-22` `externalParameters` and `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/src/org/openl/rules/ui/WebStudio.java:185-186`. Resolve the policy once per compilation into an immutable snapshot of the source module's parameters holding the effective external-parameter-first/JVM-second value: `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/engine/OpenLCompileManager.java:107-110` hands the binder that snapshot instead of the caller's map and reads external dependencies from it, and `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/lang/xls/XlsBinder.java:189-196` reads it at the start of `bind`, before `makeOpenL`/`TypeResolver` creation; the resolver, library imports, member checks and the factory's validation context all use that one value and never fall back to the JVM property; change cache key to `XLSX::<uri>::<policy>` with atomic per-key `OpenL` construction that runs only `makeOpenL`, outside dependency compilation and binding; `DEV/org.openl.rules/src/org/openl/rules/runtime/RulesEngineFactory.java:179-183` takes the snapshot under its lock, returns a cached `CompiledOpenClass` only for that snapshot's policy, and otherwise compiles with it through a new `OpenLManager.compileModuleWithErrors` overload, publishing result, policy and generated interface together and replacing only an interface it generated; a parameter change during a compilation applies at the next call; `OpenLCompileManager.processSource` holds its snapshot's policy as the thread's compilation policy, and `DEV/org.openl.rules.project/src/org/openl/rules/project/instantiation/SimpleDependencyLoader.java:69-85`, which loads every OpenL Studio and OpenL Rule Services project module as a dependency, reuses a cached dependency only when its recorded policy equals the requesting compilation's and otherwise compiles it again with that policy set after the module properties. | Enforce every `TypeResolver` return path: core registrations, aliases/imports, found cache, package lookup and dynamic loading; refuse denied-class library imports in `makeOpenL`; run one policy-aware `BindHelper` member check on declaring and return types, judging arrays by their root component type and delegating types by their delegate, before every namespace call, target and static call, call-as-field sugar, unqualified field and target-field return; constructors resolve through `TypeResolver`, and `MethodSearch` stays unchanged. Deny `ClassLoader`, `Process`, `ProcessBuilder`, `Runtime`, `System`, `Thread`, `ProcessHandle`, `ThreadGroup`, `java.lang.Class` and `java.lang.reflect`/`java.lang.invoke`/`javax.script`/`sun.*`/`jdk.*` packages. Test both states on each resolver and member-binding path, external/JVM precedence in both combinations, same-URI policy-safe reuse in both orders through the public `RulesEngineFactory` API without reflection, a dependency recompiled rather than reused under the other policy in both orders with module properties unable to change the requested policy, a controlled policy change during compilation that follows its first value until the next call, latch-coordinated same-key and different-policy concurrency, and both application propagation paths. | No directive is assigned; core compiler paths `OpenLCompileManager`, `XlsBinder`, `TypeResolver`, `MethodNodeBinder`, `IdentifierBinder`, `BindHelper`, `RulesEngineFactory` and `SimpleDependencyLoader` change, so the full `DEV/` suite must pass at the default and the Project Guide must retain the risk, switches, migration note, four LOC ranges and target release. |
| [SEC-14](#sec-14) | In the `owasp` profile declare `owasp.failBuildOnCVSS=7` and bind `failBuildOnCVSS` to `${owasp.failBuildOnCVSS}`, never a literal; set `suppressionFiles` to `dependency-check-suppressions.xml` containing only verified false positives and no-fix entries whose affected range is open-ended with no fixed release named by any source in any line, or whose `will_not_fix`, `fix_deferred` or `end_of_life` disposition covers every release line, each with `notes`/`until`. Add `--exit-code 1 --severity HIGH,CRITICAL --ignorefile .trivyignore` to Trivy, never `--ignore-unfixed`, capture the trivy image exit status instead of stopping on it, still append the step summary whenever the JSON report exists, then exit with that status, and give the Archive report results step `if: always()` so a failing scan keeps its summary and uploads its JSON report. Because the job checks out no files, write the ignore file first in the same run block from a workflow-level list of matching IDs with `exp` dates and evidence comments. | Validate suppression XML in Dependency-Check’s namespace, exact equality of both exemption ID sets, report-only collection with `-Dowasp.failBuildOnCVSS=11` exiting 0 even while Open high-score rows remain, and a Trivy scan with an unexempted HIGH or CRITICAL finding failing the job while still publishing its step summary and JSON artifact; missing fixed metadata never qualifies as no fix. | No directive is assigned; enabling either gate fails CI while CVSS ≥7.0 findings without a verified exemption remain, so recommend activation only after the final aggregate gate reports zero. Project Guide carries the four LOC ranges and the activation task. |

## 6. Operator Configuration Actions

| Finding | Owner | Action | Type |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| [SEC-18](#sec-18) | Proxy / Infrastructure | Terminate TLS at proxy/ingress, enable HSTS and never expose port 8080 directly | Operator-only |
| [SEC-19](#sec-19) | Infrastructure | Require SASL or mutual TLS on the broker; in every deployed `kafka-deploy.yaml`, set `security.protocol` with matching SASL or TLS client settings in each of `consumer.configs`, `producer.configs` and `dlt.producer.configs`, and keep the same protocol and authentication in every service- and method-level override of those maps; restrict topic ACLs to the OpenL Rule Services principal | Operator-only |
| [SEC-21](#sec-21) | Infrastructure | Enforce Git merge approvals, restrict CI credentials used by `openl:deploy` and keep `ruleservice.deployer.enabled=false` on reachable nodes | Operator-only |
| [SEC-24](#sec-24) | Infrastructure | Set a finite Jetty form-content limit and a matching proxy body-size limit; do not retain unlimited image arguments | Operator-only |
| [SEC-44](#sec-44) | Proxy | Serve a dedicated public webroot outside `/etc/nginx`, or none, and keep nginx configuration, certificate and key files out of every served path | Operator-only |
| [SEC-01](#sec-01) | Proxy | Block `/admin/` from non-operator networks until authentication protection ships | Complementary |
| [SEC-04](#sec-04) | Proxy | Add mode-compatible SameSite/Secure cookie attributes; SAML POST binding needs SameSite None with Secure | Complementary |
| [SEC-05](#sec-05) | Proxy | Apply consistent security headers and a tested CSP without leaving SSO/static chains unprotected | Complementary |
| [SEC-08](#sec-08) | IdP | Enforce password-length and passphrase policy | Complementary |
| [SEC-10](#sec-10) | IdP | Restrict privileged group names and who can assign them | Complementary |
| [SEC-13](#sec-13) | IdP / Proxy | Enforce lockout and rate-limit `/studio/` and `/services/` | Complementary |
| [SEC-16](#sec-16) | Infrastructure | Make applications reachable only through trusted proxies and overwrite client-supplied forwarding headers | Complementary |
| [SEC-17](#sec-17) | IdP / Infrastructure | Serve LDAP over `ldaps://` with trusted certificates; override `security.ad.server-url` (shipped `ldap://ldap.example.com:3268`, `STUDIO/org.openl.rules.webstudio/resources/openl-default.properties:274`) with the directory's `ldaps://` endpoint and encrypted port, and add the directory certificate's issuing CA to the truststore of the JVM that runs OpenL Studio, as `Docs/user-guides/installation-guide/configuration.md:553`, `Docs/user-guides/installation-guide/configuration.md:560-567` describe | Complementary |
